# Actual mixed history through three checkpoints, and a failed two-plane state

Status: complete JOINT UNVERIFIED analytical draft, worker max62. There is
no new accepted fact and no formal submission. This is the one analytical
round allocated by TASK.md and master_guidance1886f8b90c7bdbb9, after
completed61, with completed ceiling62 and attempted ceiling63. The original
all-horizon/all-word exact-profile problem remains OPEN.

For a nonnegative length-n physical word h, W_n(h) is the supremum of
2*(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L*||x_0-x_star||^2), over all finite dimensions,
convex differentiable functions f with L-Lipschitz gradient and a
minimizer x_star, and x_0!=x_star, under
x_(t+1)=x_t-(h_t/L)*grad f(x_t). This is the original W=2R convention.

The construction below keeps one static maximum of ten affine planes in
R^4, takes its ONE Moreau envelope with parameter1, and executes the fixed
nine-step word

    (alpha_2,alpha_1,2,alpha_2,alpha_1,1+d/a,
                                      alpha_2,alpha_1,2).          (1)

All three ordinary blocks have changing actual gradients. The third block
has positive proximal heights and mixed gradients. At its final checkpoint
the two-plane mixture formula leaves its simplex. The actual terminal
gradient is the previously visited second-stage endpoint. Thus even
clamping the mixture to its old cap is incorrect. The one correction is
to include that retained old endpoint; neither the function nor any step
in (1) changes.

This is a three-checkpoint continuation of the actual six-step calibration,
not three traversals of freshly prescribed pure arcs. A complete third
arc is present from the start, but its two non-cap planes do not become
active on this trajectory. That failure to progress into the new arc is
part of the result, not an omitted part of the word.

The arbitrary-count calculation in Section8 gives exact all-plane state
and slack transport, with its dimension and Euclidean metric. It is NOT
an unbounded-stage payoff theorem. The explicit three-checkpoint example
refutes the particular two-plane invariant state tested here; it does not
refute all states with additional history. Section9 states the remaining
inequality without advertising it as a proved or negligible interface.

The awareness records2259fcba177f68d1 and f37ec6e9db6c6b5b were read in
full. Their mathematics is rederived below where needed, rather than
used as an accepted predecessor. The accepted original-ruler fact
5b7ff4253c38b90a proves a lower witness for its specified H_m family only.
It is not an all-word result and supplies no premise for this new geometry.
Accepted1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the project convention for Q_ij; its
envelope/interpolation calculation is also proved here for this function.

## 1. Euclidean frame and exact local data

Set epsilon=1/2. Let e_0,e_1,e_2,e_3 be orthonormal, 0<r<1,
s=sqrt(1-r^2), u_0=e_0, and

    u_(i+1)=-r*u_i+s*e_(i+1), i=0,1,2.

Then ||u_i||=1 and <u_i,u_j>=(-r)^|i-j|: the new orthogonal
coordinate proves the norm identity, and repeated substitution proves
the cross products. These are actual vectors, not orthogonal coefficient
coordinates. In particular <u_0,u_2>=r^2.

Define the local quantities

    kappa=2*s/r, omega=sqrt(1+epsilon^2+2*epsilon*r),
    theta=arctan(s/(epsilon+r)), c=omega*exp(kappa*theta),
    Delta=theta/2,
    E_i(t)=cos(t)*u_i-sin(t)*e_(i+1),
    T_i(t)=-sin(t)*u_i-cos(t)*e_(i+1),
    V_i(t)=c*exp(-kappa*t)*E_i(t),
    N_i(t)=E_i(t)+kappa*T_i(t).

The curve endpoints are c*u_i and w_i=epsilon*u_i-u_(i+1).
For z,t in [0,theta], writing v=z-t gives

    <N_i(t),V_i(z)>/[c*exp(-kappa*t)]
       =exp(-kappa*v)*(cos(v)+kappa*sin(v)).

Its derivative in v is
-(1+kappa^2)*exp(-kappa*v)*sin(v); its maximum is1 at0.
Thus N_i(t) supports the whole arc and the slope0 at V_i(t).
At the cap t=0, e_(i+1) is another supporting normal. Consequently
every vector X*u_i+Y*e_(i+1) with X>=0 and Y+kappa*X>=0 supports
the cap. These statements also hold for any finite subcollection of
the arc.

Use the following exact two-step local backward path:

    A_D=exp(-kappa*Delta)*(cos(Delta)+sin(Delta)/kappa),
    B_D=exp(-kappa*Delta)*sin(Delta)/kappa,
    t_0=0, t_1=B_D, t_2=B_D*(1+A_D),
    theta_l=theta-l*Delta, R_l=c*exp(-kappa*theta_l),
    v_(i,l)=R_l*E_i(theta_l),
    r_(i,l)=R_l*((1+t_l)*E_i(theta_l)
                              +kappa*t_l*T_i(theta_l)),
    alpha_l=1-exp(-kappa*Delta)*cos(Delta)+B_D
                         +(1+kappa^2)*B_D*t_(l-1), l=1,2.        (2)

Resolving radial and tangent coordinates at theta_l gives

    r_(i,l)-r_(i,l-1)=alpha_l*v_(i,l).                          (3)

For example the tangent cancellation is t_l=B_D+A_D*t_(l-1),
and the radial component gives exactly alpha_l in (2). Both alpha_l
are positive. The proximal displacement
r_(i,l)-v_(i,l)=R_l*t_l*N_i(theta_l) is a supporting normal, so the
local chronological block is (alpha_2,alpha_1), with gradients
v_(i,2),v_(i,1),w_i and local envelope values

    R_l^2*(t_l+1/2), with proximal heights R_l^2*t_l.          (4)

Write

    r_(i,2)=A*u_i-B*u_(i+1),
    B=c*kappa*t_2/s, A=c*(1+t_2)-r*B,
    Z=A+r*B=c*(1+t_2),
    R0^2=c^2*((1+t_2)^2+kappa^2*t_2^2),
    ell=1+epsilon*r, beta=ell/(epsilon+r),
    M=A+beta*B=Z+s^2*B/(epsilon+r), a=ell/M.                  (5)

Equation(3) expresses r_(i,2) as w_i plus positive multiples of
the two earlier arc slopes. Writing V_i(t)=p*u_i-q*u_(i+1) gives
p>=epsilon*q>=0, so A>=epsilon, B>=1 and A>=epsilon*B.
All local arc pairs have positive inner product; backward additions
in(3) show ||r_(i,l)||<=R0.

The following explicit admissibility conditions are imposed on r:

    9/10<=r<1,
    1/7<alpha_1<1/5, 1/6<alpha_2<2/9.                       (6)

This is a nonempty parameter domain, defined by the exact formulas(2).
Indeed as r tends1, theta/s tends2/3, kappa/s tends2, and

    B_D -> 1/6, A_D -> 7/6, t_2 -> 13/36,
    alpha_1 -> 1/6, alpha_2 -> 7/36.                         (7)

These follow from sin z/z->1 and exp z->1, without any numerical
evaluation. All limiting alpha inequalities in(6) are strict. Thus all
r sufficiently close1 satisfy(6). No particular decimal r is asserted
to have passed an executed check.

Set

    S=alpha_1+alpha_2, zeta=(1-alpha_2)*(1-alpha_1),
    X= zeta*r-epsilon,
    D_end=2*epsilon^2+3*epsilon*r-zeta*(2+epsilon*r),
    L_end=zeta*ell-epsilon*(epsilon+r).                      (8)

The rational bounds in(6) imply

    28/45<zeta<5/7, X>3/50,
    D_end>37/20-25/14=9/140>0, L_end>0.                     (9)

For the last assertion, ell-r*(epsilon+r)=s^2>0 and zeta*r>
epsilon imply zeta*ell>epsilon*(epsilon+r).

## 2. Fully specified amplitude and one static function

The amplitude restrictions are explicit and are fixed before the run.
They will also allow a uniform bound on the bounded remainders below.
Let

    C_1=-w_0-a*r_(1,2), C_bd=omega+a*R0,
    Hpref=omega+(1+2*a)*(R0+c),
    V_0=c*(r*(a*Z-1)-epsilon),
    E_bd=4*c*epsilon*r+2*abs(V_0)/c,
    Y_bd=C_bd+(3/2)*E_bd+S*c,
    R_bd=3*E_bd+(S+1)*c,
    T_bd=R_bd+a*omega.

Define D_base to be the maximum of

    2*c, 8*c*r*epsilon, sqrt(8*c*r*a*Z), c*r/epsilon,
    [1+2*(epsilon+c)/r]/epsilon,
    [Hpref+r*ell]/(epsilon*r).

Define D_all as the maximum of D_base and the following positive or
nonnegative quantities:

    c/r^2,
    2*abs(V_0)/(c*epsilon*r),
    2*(4*c+E_bd)/zeta,
    4*S*c/(epsilon*r),
    Y_bd/(epsilon*r),
    sqrt(1+kappa^2)*Y_bd/(epsilon*s),
    c*((3/2)*E_bd+S*c)/(epsilon*(epsilon+r)),
    c*sqrt(1+kappa^2)*r*T_bd/(s*D_end),
    omega*T_bd/L_end,
    c*(C_bd+T_bd)/(X*r*(epsilon+r)),
    T_bd/zeta.                                             (10)

All denominators are strictly positive by(6),(9). For example take

    d=1+2*D_all.                                           (11)

Every assertion below holds for ANY d>D_all, including(11). There
is no later amendment to d, alpha_1, alpha_2 or any step after an
active set fails. The actual physical word is exactly(1).

Define x_6=C_1-d*w_1 and C_T=x_6-d*u_2=C_1-epsilon*d*u_1.
For any center C and scale b>0 let

    Phi_(i,b,C)(y)=max{0,b*<v_(i,l),y-C>:l=0,1,2}.

Fix before the first step the SINGLE function

    J(y)=max{Phi_(0,1,0)(y),Phi_(1,a,C_1)(y),
                                      Phi_(2,d/c,C_T)(y)},
    F(x)=min_y[J(y)+||x-y||^2/2].                           (12)

There are nine nonzero planes and the plane0. The third-stage cap
is q=d*u_2, with affine plane <q,y-C_T>. It is exactly the terminal
plane of the earlier six-step calibration. Its two additional arc
planes are present throughout this run. There is no sum of local
envelopes and no overlap-based change to the physical steps.

## 3. The first six steps, including every future plane

Their positions and gradients are

    x_0=r_(0,2), x_1=r_(0,1), x_2=w_0,
    x_3=-w_0=C_1+a*r_(1,2),
    x_4=C_1+a*r_(1,1), x_5=C_1+a*w_1,
    x_6=C_1-d*w_1,
    (g_0,...,g_5)=(v_(0,2),v_(0,1),w_0,
                                      a*v_(1,2),a*v_(1,1),a*w_1). (13)

Equation(3) and the steps2,1+d/a prove every GD update in(13).
The corresponding values and proximal heights are(4), multiplied
by1 for stage0 and by a^2 for stage1. Here is a joint check that
these are jets of the full function(12).

An arc slope written p*u_i-q*u_(i+1) has p>=epsilon*q>=0 and

    (r*p+q)/(p+r*q)<=beta.

For a stage1 slope v=p*u_1-q*u_2 one gets

    <v,-C_1>=-ell*(p+r*q)
                         +a*((p+r*q)*A+(r*p+q)*B)<=0,          (14)

because a*M=ell. Moreover <u_1,N_0(t)><0 on[0,theta]: direct
substitution gives
-r*cos t-s*sin t+kappa*(r*sin t-s*cos t)<0 since
tan t<=s/(epsilon+r)<s/r. Thus every stage1 plane is nonpositive
at every first-block proximal point, and at0.

For an old slope v=p*u_0-q*u_1, the value at the stage1 center is

    <v,C_1>=-epsilon*(p+r*q)-(r*p+q)+a*(r*p+q)*Z.            (15)

The ratio (p+r*q)/(r*p+q) is at least(epsilon+r)/ell, while
a*Z<ell and omega^2-ell^2=epsilon^2*s^2>0. These facts imply(15)<0.
At an earlier stage1 proximal point the extra term equals

    -a*(r*p+q)*R_l*t_l*(cos(theta_l)-kappa*sin(theta_l))<=0.

Indeed kappa*tan theta<=1 follows from r>=9/10>
sqrt(2/3). This checks all old stage0 planes there.

All six prefix proximal points have norm at most Hpref. Also

    <u_2,C_T>=-r*ell+a*(r*A+B)+epsilon*r*d>Hpref             (16)

by D_base. They have zero e_3 coordinate. Every stage2 plane
evaluates to

    (d/c)*R_l*cos(theta_l)*<u_2,y-C_T><0.                  (17)

Thus BOTH newly added future planes, as well as the stage2 cap,
are inactive throughout the prefix and at0. Since the appropriate
local slope is a subgradient of J at each stated proximal point,
(13),(4) are the actual prefix data. In particular J(0)=0 and
min J=0. No queried value has been changed.

## 4. Exact mixed gradients through the third ordinary block

Put

    p=c*u_0, q=d*u_2, v=p-q,
    D=||v||^2=c^2+d^2-2*c*d*r^2,
    V=c*epsilon*r*d+V_0,
    eta_0=V/D,
    eta_star=d*(d-c*r^2)/D,
    g_bar=q+eta_star*v,
    xi=eta_0-eta_star.                                   (18)

One has <v,g_bar>=0. Also g_bar is the shortest vector on the
whole affine line through p and q, so ||g_bar||<=c. Do not impose
a simplex constraint on eta_star: our choice d>c/r^2 implies

    eta_star-1=c*(d*r^2-c)/D>0.                           (19)

Define for j=0,1,2

    P_0=1, P_1=1-alpha_2, P_2=zeta,
    S_0=0, S_1=alpha_2, S_2=S,
    eta_j=eta_star+xi*P_j,
    g_(6+j)=g_bar+xi*P_j*v,
    y_(6+j)=C_T+eta_0*(q-p)-S_j*g_bar,
    x_(6+j)=x_6-S_j*g_bar-xi*(1-P_j)*v.                   (20)

These formulas are actual jets, not just a formal face recurrence.
We prove the proximal conditions against the entire dictionary.

First D>(d-c)^2>d^2/4. By(10), V>0 and

    0<eta_0*d<=E_bd,
    0<eta_star-1<4*c/d.

Hence

    0<eta_j<1-P_j+(4*c+E_bd)/d<1-zeta/2<1                (21)

for j=0,1,2, with the first upper bound also valid for j=0.
In particular xi<0. These inequalities use the bound on d in(10),
not the illegal assumption eta_star<=1.

The p-plane and q-plane tie at y_6: their difference at C_T is
<p,C_T>=V, and its change along eta_0*(q-p) is -eta_0*D.
They continue to tie at every y_(6+j), since <v,g_bar>=0.

Every such y has the representation

    y=-epsilon*d*u_1+E_y, ||E_y||<=Y_bd.                  (22)

Here ||C_1||<=C_bd, ||eta_0*(q-p)||<=(3/2)*E_bd and
||S_j*g_bar||<=S*c. In its (u_0,e_1) coordinates, (22) has
positive first coordinate and

    <e_1,y>+kappa*<u_0,y>
       >=epsilon*s*d-sqrt(1+kappa^2)*Y_bd>0.             (23)

The cap-support criterion of Section1 proves that p dominates
every other stage0 plane. Its value is strictly positive.

Every stage1 slope has u_1 component R_l*cos(theta_l)>=epsilon+r
and norm at most c. Therefore its unscaled evaluation at y-C_1 is
at most

    -epsilon*d*(epsilon+r)
                            +c*((3/2)*E_bd+S*c)<0.      (24)

For stage2, all e_3 coordinates are zero. Its radial coordinate is

    <u_2,y-C_T>
      =[V*(d-c*r^2)-S_j*c^2*d*(1-r^4)]/D>0.            (25)

Indeed V>=c*epsilon*r*d/2 and d-c*r^2>=d/2; the negative
term is at most S*c^2*d, and(10) makes the numerator positive.
At a positive pure radial normal the cap uniquely dominates the
two other arc slopes: R_l*cos(theta_l)<c for l=0,1.

Thus the p-plane and q-plane are exactly the active nonzero planes,
above0. Their convex combination in(20),(21) belongs to partial J(y),
and x-y=g. This proves all the proximal identities. It also proves
the two GD updates with the UNCHANGED ordinary steps alpha_2,alpha_1,
because 1-P_(j+1)=1-P_j+alpha_(2-j)*P_j.

The exact positive heights and values are

    H_(6+j)=<p,y_(6+j)>=V+eta_0*<p,q-p>-S_j*||g_bar||^2,
    F(x_(6+j))=H_(6+j)+||g_(6+j)||^2/2.                 (26)

The equality <p,g_bar>=||g_bar||^2 follows from p-g_bar being
parallel to v. These heights are positive by(22)-(23). At j=0
one may also write F(x_6)=d^2/2+V^2/(2D), by expanding(26).

Since 0<P_2<P_1<P_0, xi<0 and v!=0, the three gradients in(20)
are distinct. Their directions differ as well: g_bar is nonzero
and perpendicular to v because p,q are independent for r<1.
Thus the third ordinary block really has changing ordinary gradients.

## 5. The exact failed face test and its one correction

The third checkpoint is the step2 from x_8. The actual resulting
point, using the already proved g_8, is

    x_9=x_8-2*g_8
       =C_1+d*D_vec+R(d),
    D_vec=-epsilon*u_1-zeta*u_2,
    R(d)=(1+zeta)*eta_0*(q-p)+(zeta-S-1)*g_bar,
    ||R(d)||<=R_bd.                                      (27)

The norm bound follows from(21), ||eta_0(q-p)||<=(3/2)E_bd,
||g_bar||<=c, zeta<1, and |zeta-S-1|<=S+1.

If the two-plane face were invariant at this checkpoint, its next
coefficient would have to be

    eta_9^face=2*eta_star-eta_2>1.                         (28)

The inequality is strict by(19),(21). This is the FIRST failed
full-face consistency test: the coefficient of q would be negative.
No reassignment of values changes this convex-combination defect for
this fixed function and these two slopes.

Clamping to p is not the answer either. At the proposed point x_9-p,
the leading p-plane value is d*<p,D_vec>=-d*c*r*X<0,
whereas the leading stage1 endpoint-plane value is d*a*L_end>0.
All remainder terms are bounded independently of d. More directly,
the complete check immediately below proves that the actual gradient
is a*w_1, which is not on the segment[p,q]. The latter segment has
strictly positive u_0 component, while a*w_1 has u_0 component
-a*r*(epsilon+r)<0.

The ONE correction retains the whole J and word(1) and uses

    g_9=a*w_1, y_9=x_9-a*w_1.                            (29)

Here is the check against every plane, including the future ones.
In the orthonormal (u_1,e_2) frame,

    D_vec=X*u_1-zeta*s*e_2, X=zeta*r-epsilon>0.

For the continuous stage1 arc, its support value on D_vec is

    f_arc(t)=c*exp(-kappa*t)*(X*cos t+zeta*s*sin t).

Its derivative is c*exp(-kappa*t) times

    (zeta*s-kappa*X)*cos t-(X+kappa*zeta*s)*sin t.

At t=theta this bracket divided by cos(theta) is exactly
s*D_end/[r*(epsilon+r)]>0. Since X+kappa*zeta*s>0, the
derivative is positive throughout[0,theta]. Quantitatively,

    f_arc'(t)>=s*D_end/r,
    <w_1-v_(1,l),D_vec>>=(theta-theta_l)*s*D_end/r,
    ||w_1-v_(1,l)||<=c*sqrt(1+kappa^2)*(theta-theta_l)
                                                        (l=1,2). (30)

The first bound uses R(t)>=omega and cos t>=cos theta=(epsilon+r)/omega.
The norm bound integrates ||V_1'(t)||<=c*sqrt(1+kappa^2).
As ||R(d)-a*w_1||<=T_bd, the corresponding condition in(10)
and(30) prove that w_1 strictly dominates the other two stage1
slopes at y_9-C_1. Its value is positive because

    <w_1,y_9-C_1>>=d*L_end-omega*T_bd>0.                (31)

Every stage0 slope has

    <v_(0,l),D_vec>
      =-R_l*X*(r*cos(theta_l)+s*sin(theta_l))
      <=-X*r*(epsilon+r)<0.                            (32)

Its evaluation at y_9 is consequently at most
-d*X*r*(epsilon+r)+c*(C_bd+T_bd)<0 by(10).

Finally

    y_9-C_T=-zeta*d*u_2+R(d)-a*w_1.

It has zero e_3 coordinate and negative u_2 coordinate, by d>T_bd/zeta.
Every stage2 plane is therefore negative, exactly as in(17).
Equations(30)-(32) show that the stage1 endpoint is the unique active
plane at y_9. Its slope is(29), and x_9-y_9=g_9. This proves the
corrected terminal jet for the ORIGINAL nine-step word.

The exact terminal height and value are

    H_9=a*d*L_end+a*<w_1,R(d)>-a^2*omega^2>0,
    F(x_9)=a*d*L_end+a*<w_1,R(d)>-a^2*omega^2/2.          (33)

This is a return to the actual gradient queried earlier at x_5,
at a new point with a positive proximal height. It cannot be
represented as a fresh zero-height stage reset.

## 6. Smoothness, every original row, and the closest distance

J is a finite convex nonnegative maximum with J(0)=0. The objective
J(y)+||x-y||^2/2 is coercive and strictly convex, hence has a unique
minimizer y. Completing the square in the subgradient inequality
proves that its optimality condition is x-y in partial J(y); the
converse follows by a directional difference quotient.

Write g=x-y. For two such pairs, summing the two subgradient
inequalities gives

    ||g-g'||^2<=<g-g',x-x'>.

Also testing y as a trial point at x+z gives
F(x+z)<=F(x)+<g,z>+||z||^2/2, while the subgradient inequality
gives the opposite bound F(x+z)>=F(x)+<g,z>. Thus F is finite,
convex, differentiable and has1-Lipschitz gradient g.
Its minimum is0 and its minimum set is exactly

    C_min={z:<v_(0,l),z><=0,
               <v_(1,l),z-C_1><=0,
               <v_(2,l),z-C_T><=0, l=0,1,2}.            (34)

The positive scales do not affect these inequalities. This is the
whole nonempty polyhedron, not a selected minimizer only. The least
global gradient Lipschitz constant is1: both0 and p are active at
the proximal point0, so every x=t*p,0<=t<=1, has proximal point0
and gradient x. Two distinct such inputs give Lipschitz ratio1.

For every actual query, terminal point and any minimizer write
x_i=y_i+g_i, f_i=H_i+||g_i||^2/2, H_i=J(y_i). The EXACT row identity is

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
         =H_i-H_j+<g_i,y_j-y_i><=0.                    (35)

The final inequality is the subgradient inequality for J. Thus
ALL original directed interpolation rows, remote in either direction,
hold. The Euclidean Gram is positive semidefinite because all the
points and gradients are actual vectors in R^4. No sign-changing
coefficient frame is substituted for that metric.

Equation(3) gives x_0=w_0+alpha_1*v_(0,1)+alpha_2*v_(0,2).
For every z in C_min each of these three slope evaluations is
nonpositive, so <x_0,z><=0. Since0 belongs to C_min,

    ||x_0-z||^2>=||x_0||^2+||z||^2,
    dist(x_0,C_min)^2=||x_0||^2=R0^2.                   (36)

In particular0 is the unique closest minimizer even in the enlarged
R^4 function. The finite lower witness, in the required W=2R
normalization, is therefore

    W_9(h)>=
       [2*a*d*L_end+2*a*<w_1,R(d)>-a^2*omega^2]/R0^2.  (37)

For arbitrary L>0 the function L*F and steps h_t/L have the same
trajectory, minimum set, closest radius and normalized payoff.
This is a statement about(1), not about the original ruler word or
the unrestricted minimax value.

## 7. Size of the physical correction and the paid parameter regime

For fixed admissible r as d tends infinity, every threshold in(10)
stays fixed and

    eta_0=O(1/d), eta_star ->1,
    eta_1 ->alpha_2, eta_2 ->1-zeta,
    x_9/d ->D_vec,
    F(x_9)/d ->a*L_end.                                (38)

Although the first remote mixture coefficient tends to0, after only
two unchanged ordinary steps its coefficient tends to1-zeta, which
is bounded away from0. For comparison only, let x_8^pure=x_6-S*q
be the point obtained by incorrectly retaining the pure gradient q.
Equations(20) give

    (x_8-x_8^pure)/d ->alpha_1*alpha_2*u_2.             (39)

Thus the error is order d, not an infinitesimal local error. It is
created during two bounded ordinary steps, before a new large checkpoint.

As r tends1, the exact local limits are

    c,omega ->3/2, a ->36/49, R0^2 ->2401/576,
    S ->13/36, zeta ->145/216,
    X ->37/216, D_end ->139/432,
    L_end ->37/144.                                    (40)

The D_end value follows from2-(5/2)*(145/216)=139/432.
All quantities entering(10) remain bounded except its displayed
1/s factors. Their numerators and positive non-s denominators have
finite limits, and the numerator of the cap-normal bound stays
positive. Consequently D_all is of order1/s, and choosing(11)
charges d of order1/s and the physical checkpoint1+d/a of order1/s.
This is a sufficient threshold for this proof, not a lower bound on
the amplitudes required by every construction.

Along(11) as r tends1, the bounded remainders in(27) remain uniformly
bounded, and (38)-(40) yield

    eta_2 ->71/216,
    x_9/d ->-(37/216)*e_0,
    F(x_9)/d ->37/196,
    [2*F(x_9)/R0^2]/d ->10656/117649.                   (41)

Also(39) tends(7/216)*e_0. These are symbolic limits, not executed
recurrences or numerical observations. A growing d changes the
explicit family of words(1); no comparison between different d's is
presented as a correction on the same physical word. Each finite
word is fixed before its gradients are computed.

There is no B_n-scale conclusion here: n remains9. Increasing d or
letting r tend1 at this fixed horizon cannot substitute for an
unbounded-horizon, all-word comparison.

## 8. Exact full-history state and its actual cost

This section records an algebraic state for a fixed arbitrary finite
dictionary. It is included to make the missing closure precise, not
to claim that defining a proximal optimization solves the problem.

Let there be k complete finite arc stages with their centers and
scales fixed before the run. There are N=3k+1 affine planes including
the plane0, in ambient dimension k+1. Write each plane as

    L_mu(y)=<a_mu,y>+b_mu, mu=0,...,N-1,
    a_0=0, b_0=0,
    K_munu=<a_mu,a_nu>, z_mu=<a_mu,x>+b_mu.              (42)

K is the ACTUAL slope Gram. If a slope of stage i is
A_i*u_i-B_i*u_(i+1) and one of stage j>i is
A_j*u_j-B_j*u_(j+1), its inner product is exactly

    -(-r)^(j-i-1)*(r*A_i+B_i)*(A_j+r*B_j).             (43)

Within a stage the orthonormal(u_i,e_(i+1)) formula applies.
Equivalently the vector synthesized from a coefficient column t has
squared norm sum t_i^2+2 sum_(i<j)(-r)^(j-i)t_i*t_j. It is not ||t||_2^2.
The direction Gram has eigenvalues between(1-r)/(1+r) and
(1+r)/(1-r): absolute row sums bound it above, while its inverse
quadratic form on measurements m_i=<u_i,x> is

    ||x||^2=m_0^2+sum_(i=1)^k (m_i+r*m_(i-1))^2/s^2.  (44)

Expanding(44) bounds the inverse by(1+r)/(1-r) and proves the
lower bound. Formula(44) is also an exact radius charge when such
measurements, rather than x itself, are prescribed.

At a current input x, let w be any vector of active convex weights,
and H the common proximal height. Its complete equations are

    w_mu>=0, sum_mu w_mu=1,
    z-K*w<=H*1,
    w_mu*(z_mu-(K*w)_mu-H)=0 for every mu,
    g=sum_mu w_mu*a_mu, y=x-g,
    F(x)=H+(1/2)*w^T*K*w.                              (45)

These conditions are equivalent to the proximal condition:
the inequalities state that H=J(y), the complementarity says every
weighted plane is active, and g is their convex combination.
The vector g is unique even if w is not. With a physical step h>=0,
the exact next evaluation column is

    z^+=z-h*K*w.                                      (46)

For completeness, the subgradients of a finite maximum at y are
exactly the convex hull of its active slopes. A convex combination
gives a subgradient by summing the active affine lower bounds. For
the other inclusion, if g is outside that finite compact convex hull,
its closest point a separates it strictly in direction g-a. The
directional derivative of the finite maximum is the maximum of the
active slope evaluations in that direction, smaller than the
evaluation of g. This contradicts the subgradient inequality.
This justifies the necessity of the weights in(45), as well as their
sufficiency.

There are N dynamic plane evaluations, N candidate weights and one
height. Storing an unrestricted explicit Gram requires N^2 entries;
the frame and stage coefficients compress its representation but do
not delete any inequality in(45). An affinely independent active
set has at most k+2 planes. To see that some such representation
exists, an affine dependence among positively weighted slopes permits
moving the weights along a zero-sum dependence until one weight
vanishes; repeat without changing g. This is a finite reduction in
an argument, not an executed active-set search.

For a fixed affinely independent active set A, the equations for its
weights and height are the explicit linear system

    [ K_AA  1 ][ w_A ] = [ z_A ],
    [ 1^T   0 ][  H  ]   [  1  ].                     (47)

It is invertible: in its homogeneous nullspace, multiplying the first
row by w_A^T and using 1^T*w_A=0 gives
||sum w_mu*a_mu||^2=0, so affine independence gives w_A=0 and H=0.
No matrix in(47) was formed or solved by a program in this round.

There is also a useful coordinate-free form. Let P_A be the orthogonal
projector onto the span of active slope differences, and g_perp=(I-P_A)g.
As long as the proposed next point has this same active face, equality
of its plane values and the affine combination of slopes give

    g^+=g-h*P_A*g,
    y^+=y-h*g_perp,
    H^+=H-h*||g_perp||^2.                              (48)

For the first identity, the change in g lies in the difference span,
and the active-plane equality makes the change in x-g orthogonal to
that span. For the last identity every active slope has the same
projection g_perp onto its orthogonal complement.

Every retained or future plane has slack

    sigma_mu=H-L_mu(y)>=0.

Its EXACT prospective update inside this face is

    sigma_mu^+=sigma_mu
                +h*(<a_mu,g_perp>-||g_perp||^2).        (49)

The barycentric weights from(47) must remain nonnegative, AND every
one of(49) must remain nonnegative. These are the tests; dropping
old slacks gives a different, insufficient state.

For the actual face{p,q}, P_A is projection on v, g_perp=g_bar,
and(48) is exactly(20). It passes both ordinary steps by Section4.
The physical step2 fails the weight test(28). Replacing that test
by clamping fails the old endpoint's slack as well, by Section5.
Thus(46) is an exact state description with an explicitly witnessed
change of face, not an invariant two-plane transfer theorem.

The state retains the full history. Formula(43) has alternating signs
and magnitude r^(j-i-1) times the displayed coefficients. In a regime
k*s^2->0, these weights are 1+o(1) uniformly for all separations up
to k. Indeed 1-r^j<=(j)(1-r) and 1-r=s^2/(1+r).
Their absolute sum is(1-r^k)/(1-r), of order k in that regime.
This is a count of nondecaying constraints, not a proof that their
absolute sum is an unavoidable payoff loss. Cancellations have to be
proved in the actual signed state; they cannot be assumed.

## 9. Exact scope of failure and the missing inequality

The failed candidate is the positive-height two-plane carrier cone:
keep the last cap and its one reactivated old cap, transport the
mixture and common height by(48), and hope to use the same state at
successive checkpoints. It already fails on the fixed three-checkpoint
word(1). This failure uses actual changing gradients, actual positive
heights, the actual Gram, one static global J, and all future planes.
It is distinct from the old pure-stage positive cycle.

The sole correction computes the exact terminal old endpoint(29).
It does NOT establish closure of a three-plane or larger history cone
under subsequent arbitrary-stage additions. No second repair or new
mechanism is asserted.

For any proposed active-face induction, the concrete missing row
inequality is, for EVERY omitted old or future plane,

    sigma_mu >= h*[||g_perp||^2-<a_mu,g_perp>]_+,          (50)

together with nonnegative next weights. Here [t]_+=max(t,0).
Equation(50) is just the necessary and sufficient slack condition
from(49) for that proposed unchanged face; it is not claimed to hold.
The word(1) supplies an exact failed weight test and an old-plane
failure after the simplest clamp. A useful larger state must prescribe
and prove its face changes instead of applying(50) conditionally.

For newly added stages, their offsets would also have to preserve
every earlier proximal plane inequality AND a nonempty minimum set.
Keeping0 in that set and retaining x_0 as a positive combination of
planes active at0 would retain the radius argument(36), but existence
of such offsets with useful terminal payoff is not proved here.
General radius claims using only local stage radii must instead pay
the full measurement identity(44).

If an induction introduced scale slacks lambda_i>=1 to force its
constraints, the loss in a squared multiplicative transfer would be
exactly product_i lambda_i^(-2), or logarithmic loss
2*sum_i log(lambda_i). No such slack policy is introduced in this
round, and no pointwise small slack is declared globally negligible.

Even a successful arbitrary-stage construction would still require a
uniform comparison with the original ALL-split B_n=2*S_bullet(n)+1,
for every sufficiently large physical horizon and every nonnegative
word. No such B_n comparison is present. No diagnostic H_(m,tau)
specialization is claimed or has been evaluated. W=2R throughout.

## 10. Evidence status, retrieval and resource closeout

All displayed new results are part of this JOINT UNVERIFIED draft.
They have not passed fact_submit and are not facts to use downstream.
The full target remains open; the permitted conclusion is the exact
scoped failure above and the corrected finite continuation.

Matlas was queried for affine active-set Moreau structure. Its
2012.15458 Lemma2.1 is about composite Moreau differentiation, and
its other hits2006.07222 and2005.09261 do not supply the needed history
invariant. A web fallback found general polyhedral-envelope background
(including1611.00996 and1909.05795). None is an imported theorem.
The present branch stays within the fully read finite max-affine
mechanism of2259fcba177f68d1; all required envelope and face arguments
are proved above, so no new paper-dependent branch is being rejected.

Resource use: zero mathematical programs, numerical evaluations,
executed recurrences, solvers, benchmark evaluations, installations,
fact_submit calls, consult calls, or subagents. Administrative file
reads/writes, text extraction from existing JSON, serialization and
identity checks are the only local execution. No resource allowance
is renewed. The outer loop records completion after return; this
document does not claim that the process has exited.

## 11. Local notation

All notation in this draft is local except the project W_n,B_n,Q_ij.
epsilon is fixed1/2; r,s,u_i,e_i define the correlated Euclidean frame.
kappa,omega,theta,c,Delta define its support arc; E_i,T_i,N_i,V_i
are its radial, tangent, normal and slope functions. A_D,B_D,t_l,
theta_l,R_l,v_(i,l),r_(i,l),alpha_l are the explicit ordinary block.
A,B,Z,R0,ell,beta,M,a are its coefficients, radius and adjacent scale.
S,zeta,X,D_end,L_end are the explicit third-block quantities(8).
C_1,C_T are fixed centers; C_bd,Hpref,V_0,E_bd,Y_bd,R_bd,T_bd,
D_base,D_all are the exact amplitude bounds in Section2. d is the
fixed amplitude, not a dimension or an executed optimization output.
Phi,J,F are the static finite maximum and its single envelope.
p,q,v,D,V,eta_0,eta_star,g_bar,xi,P_j,S_j,eta_j define the true mixed
face; eta_star is deliberately allowed outside[0,1]. H_i are proximal
heights, g_i,x_i,y_i actual gradients, inputs and proximal points.
D_vec,R(d) give the exact terminal decomposition. C_min is the entire
minimum polyhedron. In Section8 only, k is the number of stored stages,
N the plane count, a_mu,b_mu their slopes/intercepts, K the true Gram,
z the evaluation column, m_i the frame measurements in(44), w active
weights, A an active set, P_A its
difference-space projector, g_perp the orthogonal component, and
sigma_mu the complete plane slacks. Those Section8 symbols do not
redefine earlier locally scoped A,B,P_j. lambda_i in Section9 are
hypothetical scale loss factors, not benchmark constants.

# The first landing price closes the fixed odd kink family

**JOINT UNVERIFIED — one self-contained proof draft, no new accepted fact.**

Project gd-exact-profile-20260926; worker max3; invocation85 after ended84;
completed ceiling85 / attempted86; controller cap1. Current assignment and
guidance82bfc9d550f46b42; plan06531aafba00f849.

This report carries through the prescribed FIRST mechanism: charge the first
exterior sign-crossing landing to the ORIGINAL radius, then assemble the
entire fixed odd-family bound. No second mechanism is activated. The stronger
3/2 terminal-gradient assertion is not needed and is not decided.

Every mathematical ingredient used in the proof is derived below. Earlier
drafts3f622b3ff76b77fd and a0925e826bbc6304 supplied awareness, not accepted
premises. No fact_submit or other formal call was permitted in this allocation.
The theorem below is a candidate theorem, not verifier-accepted truth.

## 1. Statement and notation

Fix one integer m>=1536, and use these SAME enclosing parameters at every depth:

    epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon, lambda=1+sqrt(2),
    c=lambda*(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
    d_j=a*c^j, h_j^lev=1+d_j,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,h_j^lev,H_j), 0<=j<m,
    S_j=sum H_j, A_j=1+S_j,
    S=S_m, D=1+2S, n=2^m-1, ell=1+a.

The n chronological physical steps are h_i, 0<=i<n. Each occurrence of a
level-j bridge has size h_j^lev. The auxiliary d_m is not an extra step.
The symbols in this report have these local meanings; in particular H_j,
G, M_j, and A_j do not redefine similarly named project-global symbols.

Choose any finite B>=kappa and any 0<kappa<=1. Put

    v=1-kappa, u=v+B,
    G(r)=min(B,max(kappa*r,r-v)), r>=0,
    g(x)=sign(x)*G(|x|), g(0)=0,
    f(x)=integral_0^x g(t)dt, F(r)=f(r), r>=0.

This is ONE fixed odd gradient law and its even objective throughout each
trajectory. Let Psi_j(s) be the actual complete H_j output starting at s, and

    M_j(R)=max_(|s|<=R)|Psi_j(s)|.

For an actual nonzero start s, R=|s| is its ORIGINAL radius, including whenever
a proof examines one of its children. The actual full-word recursion is
x_0=s, x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g(x_i), and x_n=Psi_m(s).

**Candidate whole-family theorem.** For all the parameters just specified,
and every nonzero start s,

    2f(Psi_m(s))/s^2 <= (1+21/(4m))/D < 17/(16D).       (1.1)

All original physical steps and the actual terminal are retained. The bound
is uniform in kappa, B, s, and the same enclosing m. The fixed law may have
a collapsed middle interval B=kappa or v=0.

The proof splits the parameter domain as follows.

* If kappa*(1+d_(m-1))<=1, the bound is (1+21/(4m))/D.
* If kappa*S>=12, the bound is 1/D.
* Every remaining parameter choice has
  5<kappa*S<12 and
  kappa*(1+d_(m-2))<1<kappa*(1+d_(m-1)).
  Both root children therefore preserve the core individually. For this
  entire remaining band the bound is 1/D.

The new first-landing estimate and its exhaustive use appear in Sections9-11.
Sections2-8 supply the full quantitative ingredients needed for this assembly,
without citing earlier unverified mathematics.

This is a restricted scalar function-family result. It is not a bound on the
supremum over ALL smooth convex objectives in the original PROBLEM.md. The
full-class hypothesis in accepted fact931b9653ce0f7201 remains unproved by
this report, and the original all-word minimax Goal remains OPEN.

## 2. One legal objective, its exact areas, and basic ray estimates

Integration gives the exact energy, including all knot and tail areas:

    2F(r)=kappa*r^2,                         0<=r<=1,
    2F(r)=(r-v)^2+kappa*v,                   1<=r<=u,
    2F(r)=2B*r-C_tail,                       r>=u,
    C_tail=B^2+v*(2B-kappa).                         (2.1)

At r=1 both adjacent values equal kappa; at r=u both equal B^2+kappa*v.
The first derivatives agree as well. The middle interval collapses when
B=kappa. When kappa=1, v=0 and this is the unit-curvature Huber law.

The derivative g is continuous and nondecreasing, with piecewise slopes
kappa,1,0. Partitioning any interval at the knots proves that it is
1-Lipschitz. Thus f is everywhere finite, convex, differentiable, and
1-smooth. Its unique minimizer is0, because G(r)>0 for every r>0. Its value
there is0, and its true initial distance is |s|.

For completeness the complete actual transcript is smooth-convex legal.
For x<y put Delta=g(y)-g(x), so 0<=Delta<=y-x. On [x,y],

    g(t)-g(x)>=max(0,Delta-(y-t)),
    g(y)-g(t)>=max(0,Delta-(t-x)).

Integrating each lower triangle yields Delta^2/2. These two inequalities
give every ordered interpolation row

    f_i-f_j+g_i*(x_j-x_i)+(g_i-g_j)^2/2<=0

for actual physical, terminal, and optimum samples. Repeated positions
have identical values and gradients. The scalar Gram matrix is positive
semidefinite: each quadratic form is the square of the corresponding real
linear combination of actual vectors. No terminal sample or ordered pair
is omitted.

We shall use

    0<=G(r)<=min(r,B),
    G(r)>=kappa*min(r,1).                              (2.2)

For the first inequality, kappa*r<=r and r-v<=r. For the second, inside
the core equality holds; outside the core both the cap B and the uncapped
gradient are at least kappa.

For p=G(r), monotonicity and smoothness give G(t)<=min(t,p) on [0,r].
Since p<=r, integration yields

    2F(r)<=2r*p-p^2.                                  (2.3)

For r<=u a second conversion is valid:

    2F(r)<=G(r)^2/kappa.                              (2.4)

It is equality in the core. In the middle p=r-v>=kappa, and the difference
between its right and left sides is v*(p^2-kappa^2)/kappa>=0.
This covers r=1,u and B=kappa. It is NOT asserted beyond the cap knot:
the energy there keeps growing although the gradient is constant.

A strict sign crossing means two successive nonzero iterates have opposite
signs. If a trajectory never strictly crosses and never hits zero, all
radii decrease. For a segment of total step mass T, terminal radius r,
terminal gradient p, and original segment radius R, all its queried
gradients are at least p. Therefore

    R>=r+T*p,
    2F(r)<=2R*p-(1+2T)*p^2<=R^2/(1+2T).              (2.5)

The last inequality is completion of the square. A zero hit stays zero.
In particular every full-word history without a strict crossing has score
at most1/D, including a final same-side entry from the middle or tail.

For an advertised smoothness L_adv>0, the objective L_adv*f and physical
steps h_i/L_adv yield exactly these points and this normalized payoff.
The project normalization remains mathcal W=2*mathcal R.

## 3. Same-m masses, finite margins, and the actual suffix

The enclosing parameter choices imply

    0<epsilon<1/1000, beta>999/1000,
    12/5<c<17/7<5/2,
    2/5<a<1/2, beta>c-2, c*a=beta<1.                 (3.1)

For the c bounds use 7/5<sqrt(2)<17/12 and
(29/12)*(1001/1000)<17/7. Then
a>(99/100)*(7/17)>2/5 and a<1/(12/5)<1/2.
The remaining assertions follow from these bounds.

Set eta=a/(c-2). The identity lambda*(lambda-2)=1 and c>lambda give
c*(c-2)>1>beta, hence 0<eta<1. The exact word recursion gives

    A_(j+1)=2A_j+d_j,
    A_j=(1-eta)*2^j+eta*c^j.                        (3.2)

The formula follows by substitution in the recursion and A_0=1. In particular,

    A_j>d_j/(c-2) for every j>=0,
    A_j<c^j for j>=1,
    (c-2)*d_j<A_j,
    D<2c^m=2d_m/a.                                 (3.3)

The third inequality follows from the first and c-2<1.
For L_j=S_j/d_j and j>=1,

    L_j>7/5.                                       (3.4)

At j=1, L_1=(1+a)/beta>7/5. If L_j>7/5, then
L_(j+1)=(2L_j+1+1/d_j)/c>(19/5)/(17/7)>7/5,
which proves (3.4).

The following generous finite margins will be useful:

    S>1000, d_(m-1)>9, d_(m-1)>1/epsilon,
    d_m>2/a, d_m^2>D, D/S<201/100.                 (3.5)

Indeed a>2/5 and c>2 imply d_(m-1)>2^m/5.
For integers m>=8, 2^m>20m follows from its value at8 and induction:
doubling a value above20m is above20(m+1). Consequently
d_(m-1)>4m=1/epsilon. For m>=1536 this also gives the bounds9 and1000,
using S>=1+d_(m-1). Moreover 2/a<5 and d_m>d_(m-1)>9, proving
d_m>2/a; (3.3) then gives d_m^2>D. Finally D/S=2+1/S<201/100.

At any level-k physical bridge in a complete H_j, let P be its chronological
prefix mass measured from the beginning of THAT complete word. The occurrence
has its full immediate left H_k present, so P>=S_k. Its physical suffix is

    H_k, h_(l_1)^lev,H_(l_1), ..., h_(l_t)^lev,H_(l_t),
    k<l_1<...<l_t<=j-1.                              (3.6)

To obtain this list ascend its binary word tree. Each ancestor entered
through its left child contributes its bridge and full right child;
each ancestor entered through its right child contributes nothing.
Thus (3.6) keeps every actual step and allows skipped levels without
inserting a fictitious child factor.

## 4. One-step branches and the first-crossing price

For h=1+d>=1 the positive-source signed update is

    (1-h*kappa)*r,          0<=r<=1,
    h*v-d*r,               1<=r<=u,
    r-h*B,                 r>=u.                    (4.1)

The expressions agree at r=1,u. The exact image radius of [-R,R] is

    E_h(R)=max(
       |1-h*kappa|*min(R,1),
       [d*min(R,u)-h*v]_+,
       [R-h*B]_+ ),                                  (4.2)

where [w]_+=max(w,0). Each available affine piece has its extrema at
an endpoint. The middle positive endpoint is bounded by the core
endpoint; its negative endpoint gives the second term. The tail has
its negative endpoint at the cap knot and its positive endpoint at R.
If R<1, the unavailable middle expression satisfies
dR-hv<=(h*kappa-1)R, since the difference is h*v*(R-1)<=0.
If R<u, a positive unavailable tail expression is at most the actual
positive endpoint R-h*G(R). These observations prove (4.2) also when
a formal piece is absent.

Each signed update is continuous, odd, and fixes0, so its interval image
is the entire symmetric interval specified by (4.2). Thus M_j is a
nondecreasing interval-image radius, and exactly

    M_(j+1)=M_j composed with E_(1+d_j) composed with M_j. (4.3)

These are images of actual compatible starts. No recurrence was executed.

For a strict crossing with source r and gradient p=G(r), its landing z obeys

    z=(1+d)*p-r<=d*p.                                (4.4)

For a pre-cap source r<u with a positive same-side output, necessarily
h*kappa<1, and that output lies in the core. For r<=1 this is direct
from (4.1). For 1<=r<u, the output equals
1-h*kappa-d*(r-1), which is at most1-h*kappa<1.
In either case its radius is bounded both by (1-h*kappa)*r and by
(1-h*kappa)*min(r,1). A source exactly at u may instead be classified
as saturated; its two formulas agree.

If all steps of H_j satisfy h*kappa<=1, the core [0,1] is absorbing
and its radius contracts with nonnegative factors. The full physical
core product is

    P_j(kappa)=product_(h in H_j)(1-kappa*h)
       <=exp(-kappa*S_j).                            (4.5)

The inequality follows by multiplying 1-z<=exp(-z). No suffix is dropped.

Consider a first strict crossing of a complete-word trajectory with
exterior source r>1. Its gradient satisfies r>=v+p: in the middle it
is equality, and on the tail r>=u=v+B=v+p. If its level is k and P
is its original prefix mass, all earlier radii decreased and their
gradients were at least p. Thus

    R>=r+P*p>=v+(1+P)*p>=v+A_k*p.                    (4.6)

Strict crossing also requires p>v/d_k. Put

    Q=(c-1)/(c-2)=1+1/(c-2)>10/3.

When v>0, (4.6) and (3.3) give the original-radius price

    R>v*(1+A_k/d_k)>v*Q.                             (4.7)

In a complete word whose steps all have h*kappa<=1, the first crossing
necessarily has exterior source, since any prior core entry is absorbing.
There v>0 for a nonempty word. Thus (4.7) applies to every crossing
history of such a word, even if its later first core entry is at the
very last physical step.


## 5. Joint discrete-amplitude capture at every child depth

We prove simultaneously for all j>=1 up to m:

    G(M_j(d_j*c^(-q)*B))<=a*c^(-q)*B
       for every integer q>=0,                     (5.1)

and

    any strict crossing in a complete H_j
       implies terminal gradient<=aB.              (5.2)

The law, cap B, and core knot1 remain fixed for all q. This is a discrete
amplitude grid, not an assertion for arbitrary amplitudes with B replaced.

First explain how (5.2) follows using only (5.1) at proper-child depths.
After a level-k crossing, (4.4) gives landing<=d_k*B. If k>=1, the
immediate full H_k gives terminal gradient<=aB by its q=0 grid bound.
If k=0, no H_0 steps follow locally; the landing itself has gradient
at most its radius, hence at most aB. At any later retained ancestor
l from (3.6), the incoming gradient is at most aB<B and so its source
is pre-cap. If this bridge crosses, its landing is at most d_l*aB<=d_lB,
and the full H_l again gives gradient<=aB. If it stays on the same side
with nonzero output, Section4 puts the output in the core without
increasing its gradient, and kappa*(1+d_l)<1. Every following H_l step
is then core preserving, and its gradient cannot increase. Zero is
absorbing. This accounts for the entire literal suffix in (3.6).
For H_j every child depth invoked here is at most j-1.

For the base j=1, set w=c^(-q)*B and R=d_1*w=beta*w. Since R<B,
there is no positive saturated stay. Every crossing has landing
at most a*G(r)<=a*r<=a*R. Every positive pre-cap same-side stay
has gradient at most

    kappa*[1-ell*kappa]_+*min(R,1)<=a*R,

because kappa*[1-ell*kappa]_+<=1/(4ell)<a; the last inequality follows
from 4aell>4*(2/5)*(7/5)>1. These are all cases, proving the base
grid bound (even with aR<a*w). The preceding suffix argument or (4.4)
also proves (5.2) at j=1.

Assume both conclusions through j>=1. Start the parent H_(j+1) at
any actual radius

    R<=d_(j+1)*c^(-q)*B=c*d_j*w, w=c^(-q)*B.

Write y for the actual first-child output radius, and z for the
root-bridge output radius. We partition that original bridge.

(a) A strict crossing. If q=0, G(y)<=B=w. If q>=1, the first child
receives a radius at most d_j*c^(-(q-1))*B, so its grid bound yields

    G(y)<=a*c^(-(q-1))*B=beta*w<=w.

Its bridge landing is therefore at most d_j*w. The full right H_j
and its q-th grid bound yield terminal gradient<=a*w.

(b) A saturated positive same-side stay, including y=u. The first
child could not have crossed, since (5.2) would give G(y)<=aB<B.
It also could not have hit zero. Its radii thus decreased to y>=u,
so every one of its queried gradients was exactly B. Consequently
y=R-S_j*B, and

    z=R-(A_j+d_j)*B
       <=c*d_j*w-(A_j+d_j)*B<=d_j*w.                (5.3)

For the last inequality, w<=B and A_j>(c-2)*d_j from (3.3)
give A_j+d_j>(c-1)*d_j. The complete second-child grid applies.
In particular this step does not infer all-tail behavior from a
mere bound on y; it first rules out every first-child crossing.

(c) A positive pre-cap same-side stay with no crossing in the first
child. By Section4, kappa*(1+d_j)<1 and the complete right child
stays in the core. Set t=kappa*d_j and L=L_j=S_j/d_j. Before this
bridge the first-child radii decreased, so

    R>=y+S_j*G(y)>=(1+kappa*S_j)*y.

The second inequality uses G(y)>=kappa*y at this PRE-CAP source.
No such lower bound is used for saturated large radii. By (4.5),

    p_out<=kappa*exp(-L*t)*(1-kappa-t)*R/(1+L*t)
       <=c*t*(1-t)*exp(-L*t)/(1+L*t)*w
       <=c*t*(1-t)/(1+L*t)^2*w
       <=c/[4*(1+L)]*w<a*w.                       (5.4)

Here 0<t<1, exp(Lt)>=1+Lt, and

    (1+L*t)^2-4*(1+L)*t*(1-t)=(1-(L+2)*t)^2.

Finally c/[4(1+L)]<(17/7)/(4*(12/5))=85/336<2/5<a.

(d) A positive pre-cap same-side stay with a crossing in the first
child. Again kappa*(1+d_j)<1, so both children have absorbing cores.
The first exterior crossing in the original left child pays R>vQ
by (4.7). The bridge output is at most v-t, where t=kappa*d_j,
and the complete second child has the product (4.5). Hence

    p_out<=kappa*(v-t)*exp(-L*t).

Since R<=c*d_j*w and R>vQ, one has w>vQ/(c*d_j). Therefore

    p_out/w<c*t*(v-t)*exp(-L*t)/(vQ)
       <=c*v/(4Q)<=c/(4Q)<a.                       (5.5)

Here 0<t<v, t*(v-t)<=v^2/4, and
c/(4Q)<(17/7)/(40/3)=51/280<2/5<a.

A zero bridge output stays zero. Cases(a)-(d) exhaust all other
bridge outcomes, proving (5.1) at the parent. The suffix argument
above then proves (5.2) at the parent using only proper-child
plateaus. This is a noncircular joint induction.

## 6. Global gradient control, Huber reference, and exterior histories

For 0<R<=d_jB choose an integer q>=0 such that

    d_j*c^(-(q+1))*B<R<=d_j*c^(-q)*B.

A grid boundary is taken as the upper endpoint of an interval.
The strict lower inequality and (5.1) imply

    G(M_j(R))<=a*c^(-q)*B<beta*R/d_j.               (6.1)

For R>d_jB, take any actual start |s|<=R. A crossing history has
terminal gradient<=aB<beta*R/d_j by (5.2). In a noncrossing
history with terminal radius r and gradient p, the prefix payment
in (2.5) gives |s|>=r+S_j*p>=A_j*p. Consequently

    p<=R/A_j<(c-2)*R/d_j<beta*R/d_j.               (6.2)

Zero hits give p=0. Maximizing over actual starts establishes

    G(M_j(R))<=beta*R/d_j for every R>0.           (6.3)

Every crossing history is also pre-cap at its terminal: its gradient
is at most aB<B, whereas G(r)=B exactly for r>=u. Thus its terminal
radius is strictly below u. This justifies (2.4) whenever used below.

We now derive the full Huber reference jointly. Let J_B(r) denote
the even unit-curvature Huber objective with positive-ray derivative
min(r,B). At B=1 it is the kappa=B=1 member of this same family.
Every crossing trajectory under H_m then ends with radius<=a<1
by (5.2), so its twice-energy is its terminal squared gradient.
If its original R<=d_m, (6.1) gives score<=beta^2/d_m^2<1/D.
If R>d_m, (5.2) gives score<=a^2/R^2<a^2/d_m^2<1/D.
We used d_m^2>D from (3.5). Every noncrossing history has score<=1/D
by (2.5). The scaling J_B(x)=B^2*J_1(x/B) leaves the physical word
unchanged, proving for every B>0 and every real s that

    2J_B(Psi_m^Huber(s))<=s^2/D.                   (6.4)

This scaling concerns only the auxiliary Huber reference. It does
not replace kappa,B, or the core knot of the actual kink trajectory.

Here is the exterior comparison in the scope needed for the root
argument: assume EVERY query and the terminal of an actual kink
trajectory have radius>1. No condition on inner reversal is needed.
For any of its crossing steps, (4.4) and (2.2) bound the landing by
d*min(r,B). A noncrossing positive stay from a middle source would
land at most1-h*kappa<1 by (4.1), and so is excluded by the exterior
hypothesis. Therefore every same-side stay in this history is
saturated, including a cap tie if it occurs, and equals r-hB.
Every physical step is thus bounded by

    V_(h,B)(r)=max(d*min(r,B), r-hB), d=h-1.       (6.5)

This is a nondecreasing function of r and is exactly the Huber
symmetric-interval image radius. The latter follows by maximizing
the absolute affine core output -d*x and the tail output x-hB on
[0,r]. Induction through the original physical word bounds the
actual terminal radius by the full Huber interval-image radius
from [-R,R]. The composite Huber map is continuous, so this radius
is attained by some actual start s_* in [-R,R]. Since G<=min(r,B),
integration gives F<=J_B pointwise. Thus (6.4) gives

    2F(r_n)<=2J_B(|Psi_m^Huber(s_*)|)
       <=s_*^2/D<=R^2/D.                          (6.6)

This proof explicitly establishes comparison for exterior-only
histories even when the root could reverse core points.

## 7. All starts with kappa*S>=12

A history without strict crossing is covered by (2.5). For a crossing
history, its actual terminal is pre-cap by Section6. Apply (2.4) and
(6.3) with the TRUE full-word original radius R:

    D*2F(r_n)/R^2<=D*beta^2/(kappa*d_m^2)
       =(D/S)*(beta*S/d_m)^2/(kappa*S).

From A_m<c^m=d_m/a, beta*S/d_m<c. Therefore if kappa*S>=12,

    D*2F(r_n)/R^2
       <(201/100)*(17/7)^2/12
       =58089/58800<1.                            (7.1)

This closes every history in this parameter region, with no
restriction on the number of crossings or later core visits.

## 8. No inner reversal and reduction of the remaining band

Assume first kappa*(1+d_(m-1))<=1. Every physical step preserves the
core and contracts radii there. A noncrossing history uses (2.5).
A crossing history that never enters the core uses (6.6). If a
crossing history does enter the core, (4.7) for the ORIGINAL full
word gives R>vQ and core absorption gives 2F(r_n)<=kappa, so

    2F(r_n)/R^2<kappa/(v^2*Q^2).                   (8.1)

This also covers a core entry at the terminal, with no subsequent
contraction factor assumed.

Put d=d_(m-1) and t_c=c*(c-2). The function z/(1-z)^2 increases on
(0,1). Thus kappa<=1/(1+d) gives kappa/v^2<=(1+d)/d^2. Combining
D<2c^m, a=beta/c, d=a*c^(m-1), and (c-1)^2=t_c+1 yields

    D*kappa/(v^2*Q^2)
       < [2*t_c^2/(beta*(t_c+1))]*(1+1/d).        (8.2)

For example the substitution 2c^m/d=2c/a=2c^2/beta
directly gives this coefficient. The identity lambda^2=2lambda+1
also gives

    t_c=1+2*(lambda+1)*epsilon+lambda^2*epsilon^2
       <1+8epsilon.

Indeed lambda<5/2 and epsilon<1/1000 make the coefficient of
epsilon less than7+(25/4)/1000<8. Since t_c>1 and d>1/epsilon,
the right side of (8.2) is less than

    (1+8epsilon)^2*(1+epsilon)/(1-epsilon).

The exact difference
(1-epsilon)*(1+19epsilon)-(1+8epsilon)^2
=2epsilon-83epsilon^2 is positive. Hence the last expression is
less than (1+19epsilon)*(1+epsilon)<=1+21epsilon.
Equations(8.1)-(8.2) prove

    score<=(1+21epsilon)/D                         (8.3)

throughout the no-inner-reversal region. Since
21/(4m)<=21/6144<1/16, its coefficient is less than17/16.

It remains to consider parameters with kappa*S<12 and
kappa*(1+d_(m-1))>1. For d=d_(m-1), (3.3) gives

    kappa*S>S/(1+d)>
       [c*d/(c-2)-1]/(1+d)>5.                    (8.4)

The last comparison follows from c/(c-2)>17/3 and d>9:
(c/(c-2)-5)*d>(2/3)*9=6.

Moreover (c-2)/c^2<3/40 on the parameter interval. To see this,
3c^2-40c+80 decreases there and at c=17/7 equals27/49>0.
The inequality d_m<(c-2)*(S+1) from (3.3) thus implies

    kappa*(1+d_(m-2))
       <12*[1/S+(3/40)*(1+1/S)]
       =9/10+129/(10S)<1.                         (8.5)

We used S>1000. Therefore the first inner-reversing level is exactly

    J=m-1>=1,
    kappa*(1+d_(J-1))<1<kappa*(1+d_J).            (8.6)

The original left and right H_J each have only core-preserving
physical steps. The sole physical step capable of an inner sign
reversal is the original root h_J^lev. Exterior crossings in
either child remain allowed.

Two convenient additional band bounds are

    v>49/50, D*kappa<603/25<25.                   (8.7)

Indeed kappa<12/S<12/1000=3/250<1/50, while
D*kappa=(D/S)*(kappa*S)<(201/100)*12=603/25.
These bounds use the actual S of the same full word.


## 9. New landing price measured from the ORIGINAL start

Consider the FIRST strict sign crossing of the full H_m trajectory,
assume its source is exterior, and write r for that source radius,
p=G(r), k for the physical level, P for its ORIGINAL full-word prefix
mass, and z for its positive landing radius. Equations(4.6) and (4.1)
give

    R>=r+P*p>=v+(1+P)*p>=v+A_k*p,
    z=(1+d_k)*p-r<=d_k*p-v.                       (9.1)

Every premise in this calculation is tied to the actual trajectory:
before its first strict crossing there was no zero hit and the radii
decreased; all previous queried gradients were at least p; the full
immediate left H_k occurred within the original prefix; and exterior
sources satisfy r>=v+p, including the cap knot and every tail point.
At r=u the equality r=v+p holds. There is no loss of a cap area or
replacement of the original radius by a child's radius.

Since A_k>d_k/(c-2), (9.1) implies R-v>=A_k*p>0 and

    z<=d_k*p-v
       <=[d_k/A_k]*(R-v)-v
       <(c-2)*(R-v)-v=:L_land(R).                 (9.2)

Strictness comes from the mass inequality A_k>d_k/(c-2), even if
the source is at a knot and all preceding gradients equal p.
The positivity z>0 also forces L_land(R)>0 whenever this case occurs.

Define the critical ORIGINAL radius

    Rcrit=v+(1+v)/(c-2).                           (9.3)

It satisfies L_land(Rcrit)=1. If this first crossing lands outside
the core, z>1 forces R>Rcrit. At a landing z=1, strictness in (9.2)
also forces R>Rcrit, though equality is classified as core below.

In the residual band (8.7), c-2<3/7 yields

    Rcrit>v+(7/3)*(1+v)
       =(10/3)*v+7/3>28/5.                       (9.4)

The final strict inequality uses v>49/50:
(10/3)*(49/50)+7/3=28/5. Consequently

    D*kappa/Rcrit^2
       <(603/25)/(28/5)^2
       =603/784<1.                              (9.5)

These are symbolic rational comparisons, not executed numerical
estimates or recurrence values.

There is a useful paid radius consequence. If R<=Rcrit and all steps
AFTER this first crossing preserve the core, then (9.2) gives z<1,
and the terminal radius is at most z<L_land(R). Moreover

    L_land(R)/R=(c-2)-(c-1)*v/R

increases with R>0, because v>0 in this band. Its value at Rcrit is
1/Rcrit. Therefore in this case

    r_n/R<=1/Rcrit.                               (9.6)

If R>Rcrit and a later absorbing core entry occurs, then r_n<=1,
so r_n/R<1/Rcrit as well. The phrase "later absorbing" is essential:
the root could reverse a core state; Section11 checks its location
in every case before applying this consequence.

## 10. Positive-J core-output gate at the original root

In the residual band set J=m-1, d=d_J, L=L_J=S_J/d_J, and

    t=kappa*d, q=kappa*(1+d)-1=t-v>0.
    K(z)=z for 0<=z<=1, K(z)=exp(z-1) for z>=1,
    Z=K(q)*exp(-L*t).

Here K is only a scalar comparison function, not a new objective or
gradient. Since level J-1 is nonreversing in the core,

    v<t<=c*v, q<(3/2)*v, q<(3/5)*t, L>7/5.        (10.1)

The upper bound on t follows by multiplying
kappa*d_(J-1)<=v by c. Then q/t=1-v/t<=1-1/c<3/5,
and q<= (c-1)*v<3v/2. These inequalities use the actual positive J,
which exists by (8.6), and no smaller child receives a new enclosing m.

Let y=|Psi_J(s)| be the actual terminal radius of the ORIGINAL left
child. Assume 0<y<=1; if y=0 the remainder stays zero. The root
gradient is exactly kappa*y, and its strict reversing landing is

    z=q*y<=q.

This landing is allowed to exceed1. The complete right H_J has
core-preserving physical steps. A strict crossing in that child
would require its own starting radius z>vQ by (4.7), whereas
z<=q<3v/2<vQ. Thus the complete right child has no strict crossing.
If its terminal were still exterior, all its queries would have
radii>1 and gradient at least kappa, forcing its terminal radius
to be at most z-kappa*S_J<=q-L*t<0. This is impossible. Hence it
returns to the core by its actual terminal, allowing entry on its
last step.

For a quantitative return bound, for each actual right-child step
define the auxiliary map

    C_h(r)=r-h*kappa*min(r,1).

Because 0<=h*kappa<=1 in H_J, this map is nonnegative and
nondecreasing on [0,infinity). Along the actual noncrossing child,
(2.2) gives r_next=r-h*G(r)<=C_h(r). Monotonicity thus bounds the
actual output by the chronological composition of the C_h maps
from the actual z. This compares trajectories without changing
the original function or cap.

For r>0 put b(r)=log r when r<=1 and b(r)=r-1 when r>=1.
For 0<=alpha<=1 and a positive output,

    b(r-alpha*min(r,1))<=b(r)-alpha.              (10.2)

Inside the core this is log(1-alpha)<=-alpha. Above the core with
an exterior output it is equality. For a transition from above1
to w=r-alpha<=1, it follows from log w<=w-1. A zero comparison
output and its actual bounded output stay zero.

If z>0, one has b(z)<L*t: for z<=1, b(z)<=0, while for z>1,
b(z)=z-1<q<L*t. Summing (10.2) over EVERY right-child physical
step shows that its final comparison radius is below1 and at most
K(z)*exp(-L*t). Also for 0<=y<=1,

    K(q*y)<=y*K(q).                                (10.3)

For q<=1 there is equality. If q>1 and qy<=1, use q<=exp(q-1).
If qy>=1, the function exp(qy-1)/y is increasing with y on
[1/q,1], since its derivative has the sign of qy-1; its maximum
is exp(q-1). These are all cases. Therefore the TRUE terminal
radius of H_m is bounded by

    r_n<=y*Z.                                     (10.4)

The entire right child and the actual terminal have been retained,
even when the root landing was exterior.

We now show Z<1/4 uniformly. Write e=exp(1)>2.
If q<=1, (10.1) gives L*t>7q/3, so

    Z<q*exp(-7q/3)<=3/(7e)<1/4.

The maximum follows by differentiating w*exp(-7w/3) for w>=0;
the last comparison uses e>2 and 3/14<1/4.
If q>1, then

    Z=exp(q-1-L*t)<exp(-1-4q/3)
       <exp(-7/3)<1/4,

since e>2 gives exp(7/3)>e^2>4.

Only the original-radius payment remains. If the original left
H_J had no strict crossing, its radii decreased to y, and
G(y)=kappa*y. Thus

    R>=y+S_J*kappa*y=(1+L*t)*y.

In this band L*t>(7/5)*(49/50)>1, so y/R<1/2.
Using (8.7), (10.4), and the exact core energy gives

    D*2F(r_n)/R^2
       =D*kappa*r_n^2/R^2<25/(16*4)=25/64<1/2.  (10.5)

If the original left child DID strictly cross, its absorbing core
forces its first crossing to be exterior. Equation(4.7), measured
from the ORIGINAL R, gives

    R>vQ>(49/50)*(10/3)=49/15>3.

Since y<=1, now y/R<1/3, and

    D*2F(r_n)/R^2<25/(16*9)=25/144<1/2.          (10.6)

Thus the entire y<=1 branch has score below1/(2D). This rederives
the precise positive-J core-output gate needed here. There are
no later ancestors after the original root; (10.4) is already the
actual original terminal, not an intermediate return surrogate.

## 11. Exhaustive closure when the root source is exterior

Remain in the band (8.4)-(8.7), and now assume

    y=|Psi_(m-1)(s)|>1.

A zero hit anywhere would force y=0 if before the root, or terminal
zero if later, so zero histories have already been settled.
Histories with no strict crossing anywhere have score<=1/D by
(2.5). In the rest of this section a strict crossing occurs.

The original left child cannot ever have entered the closed core,
because all its physical steps preserve it and its terminal y
is exterior. Hence every point up through the root source is
exterior.

Case A: a strict crossing occurred in the original left child.
Its FIRST crossing, which is also the full trajectory's first,
has exterior source and exterior landing z>1. Section9 therefore
gives R>Rcrit. If the root output or any later point enters the
closed core, all remaining steps lie in the right H_(m-1) and
preserve that core. Thus r_n<=1, and

    D*2F(r_n)/R^2<=D*kappa/R^2
       <D*kappa/Rcrit^2<603/784<1.               (11.1)

If neither the root output nor any later point enters the core,
the entire original history, including all queries and its
terminal, stays exterior. Apply the unrestricted exterior
comparison (6.6) to get score<=1/D. This includes every repeated
exterior crossing.

Case B: the left child had no strict crossing. The first crossing
occurs at the root or somewhere in the right child. Its source
is exterior. At the root this follows from y>1. In the right
child, any previous core entry (at the root output or earlier
in that child) would be absorbing and would forbid this strict
crossing. This proves the exterior-source premise without
assuming that a generic root step preserves core points.

Every step AFTER this first crossing belongs to the right child
and is core preserving. If R<=Rcrit, (9.2) gives its landing
z<L_land(R)<=1, and (9.6) gives r_n/R<=1/Rcrit.
The true terminal lies in the core, so

    D*2F(r_n)/R^2<=D*kappa/Rcrit^2<603/784<1.      (11.2)

The endpoint R=Rcrit is included: the landing is strictly below1.

If R>Rcrit and some point from this first crossing onward enters
the closed core, it stays there and r_n<=1. Equation(11.1) again
applies. If there is no such entry, then the entire trajectory
is exterior: before the first crossing it could not have
entered an absorbing right-child core, and the original left
child was already known exterior. The exterior comparison
(6.6) yields score<=1/D.

These cases exhaust y>1. In particular a saturated same-side
root step which enters the core and has no earlier crossing
cannot have a later crossing; it belongs to the direct ray
case(2.5). A same-side entry on the last physical step belongs
to the crossing-and-core case if there was an earlier crossing,
and otherwise to (2.5). No post-entry factor is inserted when
the entry is terminal.

All boundaries are included. Core means radius<=1, so the tie1
is absorbed in a core-preserving child. The cap knot u has the
same value, gradient, update, and area on both adjacent formulas.
When B=kappa, u=1 and the empty middle causes no missing branch.
A zero landing stays zero. The strict mass margin in (9.2)
survives source and cap ties.

## 12. Whole-family assembly and exact limitations

Every allowed parameter choice belongs to the no-inner-reversal
region of Section8, the curvature-mass region of Section7, or the
residual root-only band. In that band the actual left-child
terminal is either y<=1, covered by Section10, or y>1, covered
exhaustively by Section11. Thus every actual start has the bound
(1.1). This proves the stated whole-family theorem as a JOINT
UNVERIFIED candidate.

The proof controls the actual final objective, with all core,
middle, and tail areas retained in (2.1), and with the original
denominator s^2. It uses no amplitude substitution for B, no
reset enclosing m, no omitted physical suffix, and no full-depth
claim inferred merely from a short-child numerical test.

The new root landing-price argument survived this textual case
and boundary screen. No exact legal failure of its landing
inequality or exhaustive partition was identified, so the
conditional second mechanism was not opened. The stronger
3/2 terminal-gradient assertion remains undecided, and its
earlier failed uniformly widened-child induction is not used.

This candidate is a mathematical upper bound for the specified
fixed odd family only. It does not bound the complete
finite-dimensional smooth-convex supremum W_n(H_m). Consequently
it does not discharge the FULL-function-class hypothesis of
accepted fact931b9653ce0f7201, does not establish a negative answer
to the original minimax problem, and does not establish the
positive matching lower bound either. PROBLEM.md and the
